- Output schema (`ScoringOutput`/`ScoredStructure`) is fully generic and adequate as-is for both — no new output fields needed; `structure_path` is always hardcoded `None`, `ddg`==`total_score`, and `score_breakdown` content differs only cosmetically (`fold_values`/`n_folds` for baddg vs `trial_values`/`n_trials` for stabddg).
- Gaps worth encoding as Pydantic constraints during migration: baddg's `n_folds` has a container-enforced 1–3 range not checked host-side; stabddg's `mc_samples` must be a positive int (container-enforced only); stabddg has zero host-side validation on `mc_samples`/`noise_level`/`batch_size`/`trials`/`seed`/`device`.
- `config["chains"] = input_data.extra["chains"]` uses direct dict indexing (not `.get`) in both runners, relying on validation having already run — fragile pattern that a typed field would eliminate entirely.</result>

Summary of wrinkles:

- **boltz1 vs boltz2**: They are near-identical today — same runner, same image_tag (`boltz:1.0.0`), same shared `StructurePredictionInput`/`StructurePredictionOutput` (no per-tool subclass exists, unlike ESM's `ESM2Input`), same `config.json` shape and same `_CONSUMED_EXTRA_KEYS`. The *only* wired distinguishing signal is `self.tool_name` feeding a trivial identity dict (`_MODEL_CONFIG = {"boltz1": "boltz1", "boltz2": "boltz2"}`) that sets `config["model"]`. Registry-level differences are just `default_timeout` (3600 vs 7200), `description`, and `notes` (boltz2 documents 3 affinity-only `extra` keys: `sampling_steps_affinity`, `diffusion_samples_affinity`, `method`) — good candidates for a boltz2-only input subclass, mirroring how `ESM2Input` adds `checkpoint`.
- **rfd3 is mechanically harder than ESM's single-mode pattern** for two reasons: (1) it has **no `_CONSUMED_EXTRA_KEYS`/`_apply_extra` mechanism at all** — `extra` is flat-merged unconditionally (`config.update(input_data.extra)`), so migration must introduce the shadow-rejection pattern from scratch; (2) `design_specs: dict[str, dict[str, Any]]` is a large, genuinely untyped, tool-native mini-language (contig strings, `select_*` fields that accept bool|str|dict, symmetry, ligand codes, etc.) whose real validation lives in the container's bash `validate_config.sh`, not Python — it resists simple typed-field promotion and will likely need to remain an escape-hatch dict.
- Both `StructurePredictionInput` (boltz) and `StructureDesignInput` (rfd3) currently have **zero `ui()`/`x-autobio` hints**, unlike ESM's schemas — every field needs hints added from scratch.
- `DesignedStructure.evaluation_metrics` has a dead/aspirational docstring referencing a `mode='generate'` concept that doesn't exist anywhere in `RFD3Runner` — flagged as a design question (single-mode vs. anticipating a future design+evaluate pipeline mode).
- Boltz's `sequences: dict[str, str]` is a `SequenceSet` candidate but values can be DNA/RNA/SMILES/empty placeholders (not pure protein), so a naive FASTA-based `GenericSequenceSet` normalizer may not fit cleanly — flagged for design review rather than assumed.</result>

Summary of notable wrinkles:

- **Templates/MSA are host-validated + copied but never wired into config**: chai1 copies template files into `inputs/` and validates their existence, but no config key ever points to them; openfold3 has the same gap for both `templates` and `msa_paths` (chai1's `msa_directory` is at least wired via a config key; openfold3's `msa_paths` is not wired at all).
- **Raw-override None-value footguns**: chai1's `chai_fasta` uses presence-check in validation but truthiness-check in `prepare_workspace` (empty-`sequences` + `chai_fasta=None` slips through to produce a near-empty FASTA); openfold3's `query_json` is presence-checked consistently but a `None` value gets `json.dumps`'d into the literal string `"null"`, silently discarding any valid `sequences` also provided — a real correctness gap, not just style.
- **`parse_output` inconsistency**: esmfold requires `data["confidence"]` (KeyError if absent) while chai1/openfold3 default to `data.get("confidence", {})`.
- **Extra-key clobber risk**: chai1's/openfold3's `num_diffn_samples`/`num_diffusion_samples` (derived from `num_models`) aren't in `_CONSUMED_EXTRA_KEYS`, so a caller could override them via `extra`.
- **Chain ordering asymmetry**: chai1 sorts chain IDs alphabetically when building its FASTA; openfold3 preserves dict insertion order when building its query JSON.
- **Extra-key surface area**: esmfold has no `_CONSUMED_EXTRA_KEYS` at all (0 documented extra keys, full flat-merge); chai1 has 4 consumed + 7 documented pass-through keys; openfold3 has 4 consumed + 8 documented pass-through keys — chai1/openfold3 are meaningfully harder to migrate than the plain esm single-mode pattern given the structural (non-scalar) extras (`entity_types`, `constraints`/`query_json`, `msa_directory`/`msa_paths`, `non_canonical_residues`).
- **Shared boilerplate**: `_resolve_container_path` is copy-pasted verbatim in all three runners — a dedup candidate. Output schema itself shows no variance across the three (all share `StructurePredictionOutput`, and none populate the `affinity_*` fields on `PredictedStructure`).</result>

The pre-existing `test_apply_extra_rejects_shadowing_typed_field` (line 128-133) uses `config={}`, isolating the typed-field clause. <result>I have completed a thorough review. I traced every config.json contract by hand against the pre-migration code, verified the shared-schema invariants, and confirmed the deferred integration-test breakage. Here is my assessment.

---

## Code Review: Tools→Modes Plan 4a — SCORING singleton migrations

### Strengths

- **Byte-compat is genuinely preserved.** I traced each of the four `prepare_workspace` config dicts against the pre-migration `extra.get(...)` code path key-by-key, value-by-value, and in insertion order:
  - `baddg`/`stabddg`: `mutations` comma-join (`",".join(input_data.mutations)`), `chains` passthrough, checkpoint constants, `output_dir`, and all numeric/string params map identically. Defaults (`n_folds=3`, `mc_samples=20`, `batch_size=10000`, `trials=1`, `seed=0`, `noise_level=0.1`, `device="auto"`) match the old `.get(...)` fallbacks.
  - `antipasti`: `modes` default `"all"`, plus `checkpoint_path`/`antipasti_dir` constants — identical.
  - `prodigy`: the `chain_selection`→`"selection"` remap is preserved (`src/autobio/tools/prodigy.py:~40`), `distance_cutoff=5.5`/`contact_list=False` defaults match. The full-dict test at `tests/unit/test_prodigy.py` (`test_full_config_byte_compat`) explicitly locks the remap.
  - All four ship exact full-dict equality tests (baddg/stabddg use non-default values, which is the stronger form).
- **`_apply_extra` strengthening is correctly wired.** The collision check runs against `config` *after* all derived keys are inserted (it is the last statement in every `prepare_workspace`), `run()` sets `current_mode` before `prepare_workspace` (`src/autobio/tools/base.py:129` → `:136`), and `BaseInput.extra` always defaults to a dict (`src/autobio/schemas/base.py:30`), so the `for key in input_data.extra` scan is safe. The second clause (`key in config`) genuinely closes a real footgun that existed for `pdb_path`/`checkpoint_path`/`output_dir` (non-typed derived keys the old flat-merge would have silently overwritten). No legitimate workflow is broken — all now-blocked keys are container-internal paths.
- **`ScoringInput` is genuinely untouched** (no diff lines; only additive `BAddGInput`/`StaBddGInput` appended) and remains consumed by openmm, ligandmpnn_packer, evoef2, antifold, esm_if1, rosetta.
- **New input classes are correctly scoped.** `BAddGInput`/`StaBddGInput` subclass `BaseInput`; `AntipastiInput`/`ProdigyInput` subclass the affinity bases. Tool-specific fields (`modes`, `distance_cutoff`, `contact_list`) are added on the *subclasses*, not the shared bases. The shared bases (`BindingAffinityInput`, `ProteinBindingAffinityInput`) have no consumers other than their one tool each plus schema exports, so the added `x-autobio` hints on base fields are safe and non-serializing.
- **Flat entries fully removed** (no `TOOL_REGISTRY[...]`/`ToolEntry`/`_CONSUMED_EXTRA_KEYS`/`input_format`/`supports_batch` left in the four modules), still present in `TOOL_RUNNERS`, disjointness guard intact.
- **Consistent migration** across all four (single `predict` mode, `default_mode="predict"`, keyword tuples, display names, `&lt;NAME&gt;_TOOL` constant + docstring + `register(...)`).
- **Good test future-proofing:** `test_run_rejects_mode_for_legacy_tool` no longer depends on `prodigy` staying legacy — it registers a `fakelegacytool` and the autouse fixture now snapshots/restores `TOOL_REGISTRY` symmetrically with `CATALOG`. Validation messages were corrected to drop stale "extra dict" wording, and the matching tests were updated in lockstep.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- **Stale integration tests are guaranteed-broken, not merely un-run.** `tests/integration/test_baddg_integration.py:66`, `:108`, `tests/integration/test_stabddg_integration.py:68`,`:114`, `tests/integration/test_antipasti_integration.py:74`, `tests/integration/test_prodigy_integration.py:76`,`:116` still construct the old base classes (`ScoringInput(... extra={"mutations":..., "chains":...})`, `BindingAffinityInput(...)`, `ProteinBindingAffinityInput(...)`) and call `runner.run(...)`. Every runner now begins with `assert isinstance(input_data, BAddGInput/StaBddGInput/AntipastiInput/ProdigyInput)` — a `ScoringInput` is not a `BAddGInput`, so these will raise `AssertionError` (and then `AttributeError` on `input_data.mutations`) the moment the Docker suite runs. **Why it matters:** spec §10 permits *deferring the run*, but the fix requires no Docker — it is the identical mechanical transform already applied to the `*_e2e.py` files (swap the input class, hoist `extra` keys to top-level fields). Leaving them is a latent failure that will look like a regression to the next person who runs `pytest -m docker`. **How to fix:** apply the same input-class swap in the four integration files (or, at minimum, file a tracked follow-up issue and note it in the PR description). I lean toward doing it in this PR since it is Docker-free to write.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- **`AntipastiInput.modes` field name shadows the Tool-level `modes` concept.** `src/autobio/schemas/binding_affinity.py` adds a field literally named `modes` to a `Tool` whose mode dict is also `.modes`. No functional collision (different objects/namespaces; `_apply_extra` treats it correctly as a typed field), and it matches the container's config key, so changing it would break byte-compat — but it is a readability trap worth a code comment.
- **New numeric bounds are a (benign) behavior tightening.** `n_folds` gains `ge=1, le=3`; `mc_samples`/`batch_size`/`trials` gain `ge=1`; `distance_cutoff` gains `gt=0`. The old `extra.get(...)` path passed any value through to the container. This is per the plan's contract tables and aligns with the fail-fast guidance, and all defaults are in-range so there is no byte-compat regression — but out-of-range values that previously reached the container now raise a Pydantic `ValidationError` at construction. Intentional and positive; just flagging the semantics change.
- **Registration test asymmetry:** `baddg` and `stabddg` registration classes lack the `test_timeout` assertion that `antipasti`/`prodigy` have; their `default_timeout=600` is only covered indirectly. Cheap to add for parity.
- **`antipasti` full-dict test uses only defaults** (`modes="all"`); the non-default integer path is covered separately by `test_modes_int_override`, so coverage is adequate, but a single full-dict assertion with a non-default `modes` would be marginally stronger (matching the baddg/stabddg pattern).

### Recommendations
1. Update the four `tests/integration/test_*_integration.py` files with the same input-class swap used in the `*_e2e.py` tests (Docker-free edit), or file and reference a tracking issue in the PR.
2. Add a one-line comment on `AntipastiInput.modes` clarifying it is the DCCM normal-mode count, distinct from `Tool.modes`.
3. (Optional) add `test_timeout` to the baddg/stabddg registration tests for parity.

The only substantive gap is the four stale Docker-gated integration tests, which are guaranteed-broken and fixable without Docker; update them (or explicitly track the deferral) before merge.</result>

# Code Review: Tools→Modes Plan 4b — Structure-Prediction Singleton Migrations

### Strengths

- **Byte-compat is genuinely preserved and genuinely tested.** `_build_boltz_yaml` (`src/autobio/tools/boltz.py:150-225`) is behaviorally identical to the pre-migration version — the only change is reading `input_data.entity_types/constraints/properties/modifications/msa_paths/templates` instead of `input_data.extra[...]`. The defaults match exactly (`entity_types` default `{}` via `default_factory=dict` == old `extra.get("entity_types", {})`; `msa_paths`/`constraints`/etc. default `None` == old `extra.get(...)`). The config-assembly ordering is preserved, so JSON output is byte-identical.
- **The byte-compat pins are real, not vacuous.** Full-dict `config.json` equality tests for esmfold (`test_config_full_dict_equality`) and boltz (`test_config_full_dict_equality_num_models_1` and `..._with_overrides`, the latter exercising `diffusion_samples` mapping + `use_msa_server=False` + extra CLI-knob merge), plus a full-string generated-YAML equality test (`test_generated_yaml_equality_protein_ligand`) over a representative protein+ligand+constraints input that flows through `_build_boltz_yaml`. These would catch any drift in key ordering or entity construction.
- **Structural-key promotion is complete and correct.** Every promoted key (`entity_types`, `boltz_yaml`, `msa_paths`, `constraints`, `properties`, `modifications`, `use_msa_server`) is now read from the typed field; none is still read from `extra`; none is flat-merged into config (they're typed fields, so `_apply_extra` rejects them if passed via `extra`). The `_CONSUMED_EXTRA_KEYS` filter set is correctly deleted. `use_msa_server` promotion preserves the old override semantics exactly (default `True`, `use_msa_server=False` overrides), and the shadow-rejection tests confirm all four promoted keys now raise if smuggled through `extra`.
- **Invariants honored.** `StructurePredictionInput` is untouched (only additive module-level imports); confirmed still consumed by `chai.py` and `openfold3.py`. esmfold adopts `GenericSequenceSet` (generic flavor, so protein validation stays in `_validate_inputs` — verified by `test_fasta_text_input_normalizes` + `test_invalid_sequence_raises`); boltz keeps `dict[str, str]` per the DNA/RNA/SMILES-safety rationale. boltz1/boltz2 are two Tools sharing one `BoltzRunner`, distinguished by `_MODEL_CONFIG[self.tool_name]` ([[feedback-model-tools]] respected).
- **Commit 35e5382's fix landed correctly.** The dropped Boltz YAML guidance (bond/pocket/contact examples, multi-chain `id: [A, B]` idiom, native-schema link) is now in the `constraints`/`entity_types`/`boltz_yaml` field descriptions, which *are* rendered in `autobio info` JSON via `input_schema.model_json_schema()`.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
1. **`Mode.notes` is not rendered by `format_tool_info_catalog` — `src/autobio/cli/formatters.py:212-273`.** Confirmed accurate: the catalog formatter emits `input_schema`/`output_schema`/timeouts/hints but never `mode.notes`, in either JSON (lines 223-235) or TABLE (lines 266-272). The legacy `format_tool_info` *did* render `entry.notes` in both formats (lines 185-186, 204-206). Guidance that lives only in notes and is **not** duplicated in field descriptions is therefore invisible in `autobio info`: ESMFold's ~1200-residue limit and 16-24 GB GPU memory note; Boltz's `affinity_value → kcal/mol` conversion formula (`(6 - value) * 1.364`), GPU-memory/`max_parallel_samples` guidance, and CLI-knob defaults.
   - **Why it matters:** silently drops agent-facing operational guidance across the whole refactor.
   - **Assessment / how to fix:** This is *cross-cutting and pre-existing* — it originates in the earlier catalog formatter, and 4b merely follows the established esm/freesasa pattern (and deliberately mitigated the highest-value boltz guidance by moving it into field descriptions). It should **not block 4b**. Fix belongs in the contracts/teardown plan: either add a `notes` key to `format_tool_info_catalog` (JSON + TABLE) to restore parity, or amend Spec §8 to declare `notes` internal-only and migrate any remaining load-bearing guidance into field descriptions. Recommend deferring, with a tracked follow-up.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. **Verified non-issue (worth noting for the record):** `_apply_extra` (`base.py:200-232`) now rejects an `extra` key that collides with an already-set config key. So the contradictory input `num_models&gt;1` *and* `extra["diffusion_samples"]` raises, whereas old code silently let `extra` win. This is a fail-fast *improvement* on a degenerate input, not a byte-compat regression for any sane input. No action needed.
2. **Verified non-issue:** `_build_boltz_yaml` optional sections changed from `if section in extra` (key-presence) to `if value is not None`. These diverge only for the degenerate `constraints=None`-explicit case (old could emit `constraints: null`); for all real inputs (list or absent) output is identical, and the typed-field `None`-means-absent semantics are cleaner. No action needed.

### Recommendations
- File the `Mode.notes` rendering gap as a tracked item for the contracts/teardown plan (it affects every migrated tool, not just these three). When addressed, add a formatter-level test asserting notes appear in `autobio info` JSON so the regression can't recur silently.
- Consider (teardown-era, optional) adding one passthrough byte-string pin for `boltz_yaml` provided as a *string* (currently only the dict-passthrough and generated paths are string-pinned) — low priority since that path is a verbatim write.

### Assessment
**Ready to merge?** Yes

**Reasoning:** The migration achieves its stated success criterion — byte-for-byte-compatible `config.json` and generated `input.yaml`, backed by genuine full-dict/full-string equality pins — with the promotion complete, `StructurePredictionInput` untouched, and the two-Tools-one-runner pattern correct. The only substantive finding (unrendered `Mode.notes`) is a pre-existing cross-cutting formatter gap that 4b did not introduce and partially mitigated; it is appropriate to defer to the contracts/teardown plan rather than block this PR.</result>

## Review: Tools→Modes Plan 4c (chai1 + openfold3 migrations + notes rendering)

### Strengths

- **Byte-compat is genuinely preserved and rigorously pinned.** Both `_build_fasta` (chai1) and `_build_query_json` (openfold3) are behaviorally identical to pre-migration — only the source of the values changed (`extra[...]` → typed field). The critical iteration-order asymmetry is intact: chai1 keeps `for chain_id in sorted(input_data.sequences)` (chai.py:173) while openfold3 keeps insertion order `for chain_id, sequence in input_data.sequences.items()` (openfold3.py:163). Config-dict key insertion order is unchanged in both runners (hardcoded keys → `num_diffn(usion)_samples` → optional paths → `_apply_extra` last), so serialized `config.json` is byte-identical. The full-dict equality tests (`test_config_full_dict_equality_minimal`/`_with_overrides`) and the full-string/full-dict generated-input tests (`test_generated_fasta_equality_protein_ligand`, `test_generated_query_json_equality_protein_ligand_noncanonical`) are strong, exact pins — not smoke tests.

- **Footgun resolutions are consistent across validate + prepare.** chai1 uses truthiness in both `_validate_inputs` (`has_chai_fasta = bool(input_data.chai_fasta)`, chai.py:216) and prepare (`input_data.chai_fasta or self._build_fasta(...)`, chai.py:69). openfold3 uses `is not None` in both `_validate_inputs` (openfold3.py:232) and prepare (openfold3.py:69). This matches the pre-existing truthiness (chai) vs presence (openfold3) semantics, and only changes the degenerate untested cases — verified by `test_empty_sequences_with_chai_fasta_none_raises` and `test_query_json_none_builds_from_sequences` (the latter explicitly asserts `content != "null"`).

- **Promotion is complete.** Zero residual `.extra` reads remain in either runner (grep confirmed). The override booleans preserve their const defaults exactly: chai `use_msa_server=True`/`use_esm_embeddings=False`, openfold3 `use_msa_server=True`/`use_templates=True`/`pae_enabled=True` — all confirmed by the default-value tests and full-dict equality.

- **`StructurePredictionInput` is untouched** — the schema diff is purely additive (two new classes appended); no deletions. Both new classes correctly keep `sequences: dict[str, str]` (not `GenericSequenceSet`), preserving the overloaded protein/DNA/RNA/SMILES semantics.

- **templates/msa "copied-but-unwired" behavior is preserved with explicit negative assertions.** `test_templates_copied` now asserts `"templates" not in cfg and "template_path" not in cfg` for both tools — locking in the pre-existing no-op rather than accidentally "fixing" it.

- **Shadow-rejection coverage is thorough** and leans on the uniform `_apply_extra` mechanism (base.py:224-231): chai covers entity_types/use_msa_server/chai_fasta/constraints; openfold3 covers all 7 promoted fields. `current_mode` is correctly set before `prepare_workspace` in the real `run()` path (base.py:129 → 136), and the test fixtures set it directly to exercise `prepare_workspace` in isolation — matching the merged boltz pattern.

- **Task 1 (notes rendering) is cleanly additive.** JSON adds `"notes"` to both the tool dict and each mode dict; TABLE renders tool-notes and per-mode notes conditionally. Existing info-snapshot tests check specific keys, not exact key sets, so the additive key is safe (controller confirmed all 1376 pass).

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

- **Tool-level `notes` rendering is untested and currently unexercised.** `src/autobio/cli/formatters.py:415` (JSON `"notes": list(tool.notes)`) and formatters.py:434-435 (TABLE `if tool.notes:`) are only reachable when a `Tool` sets top-level notes; every tool in the codebase attaches notes to `Mode`, not `Tool` (`Tool.notes` defaults to `()`, catalog.py:56). The test helper `_tool_for_info()` only sets *mode* notes. Low risk (defensive, additive), but it is a genuine untested branch. Fix: add one assertion to the existing formatter tests setting `notes=(...)` on the `Tool(...)` in `_tool_for_info()` and asserting it renders, or accept as-is given `Tool.notes` is effectively vestigial.

- **openfold3 `msa_paths` note text is mildly misleading given copied-but-unwired behavior.** `_OPENFOLD3_NOTES` (openfold3.py:305-306) tells agents to "provide pre-computed MSAs via the 'msa_paths' field," but `msa_paths` files are copied into `inputs/` and never wired into `config.json` or `query.json` — so they have no effect. This is the deliberately-preserved pre-existing behavior (already a known-deferred follow-up), but the note actively advertises a no-op. Fix (follow-up): either wire `msa_paths`/templates into the query or soften the note; do not fix in this PR per plan scope.

- **`_apply_extra` collision-rejection tightens behavior slightly beyond the documented footguns.** Because `_apply_extra` rejects any extra key already present in `config` (base.py:225), an input like `extra={"num_diffn_samples": 99}` now raises, whereas the old unfiltered flat-merge would have silently overridden the `num_models`-derived value. This is an improvement (fail-fast, consistent with boltz) and only affects rare/undocumented inputs, but it is a behavior change slightly broader than the "only degenerate inputs" framing in the plan. Note only — not a regression, and this collision logic is pre-existing base-class behavior, not introduced here.

- **`_resolve_container_path` duplication** across esmfold/chai1/openfold3/boltz — already correctly triaged as teardown cleanup. No action.

### Recommendations

1. Merge as-is; optionally land the one-line tool-notes assertion (Minor #1) since it closes the last untested branch touched by this PR at near-zero cost.
2. File the two known follow-ups explicitly if not already tracked: (a) wire-or-document `msa_paths`/templates copied-but-unwired; (b) teardown removal of `StructurePredictionInput` + `_resolve_container_path` hoist.

Only cosmetic/untested-branch minors remain, none blocking.</result>

---

### Strengths

- **Byte-compat is airtight and well-locked.** In both runners the config-building blocks are unchanged (only `config.update(extra)` / `_CONSUMED_EXTRA_KEYS` loop → `_apply_extra`). The new `MPNNInput` field defaults exactly match the `InverseFoldingInput` fields they replace (`num_sequences=1`, `temperature=0.1`, `chains_to_design=None`, `fixed_positions=None`), and the packer defaults match the old `extra.get(..., _DEFAULT_*)` values (4/3/16/True/True). Field-order differences in `MPNNInput` are irrelevant since config is built by explicit key assignment.
- **The full-dict equality tests are the right tool and are genuinely strong.** They pin the exact `config.json` including key *presence and absence* — proteinmpnn minimal, ligandmpnn with `designed_chains` + flat-merged extra, and the `fixed_positions`→`fixed_residues` mutual-exclusivity case (asserting `designed_chains` is gone). Packer has both a full custom config and a minimal-defaults config pin. This is exactly how to guard byte-compat.
- **`_apply_extra` adopted correctly.** Both runners call it last, after all runner-derived keys are in `config`. It rejects collisions with typed fields *or* already-present config keys, replacing the old silent overwrite (mpnn) / silent skip (packer) with fail-fast — a real hardening. `current_mode` is wired by `run()` (base.py:129) so it works in production; the unit tests correctly set `current_mode` manually because they call `prepare_workspace` directly, bypassing `run()`. CLI knobs (`omit`/`seed`/`bias`/`temperature_per_residue`/`atomize_side_chains`, packer `seed`) still flat-merge, and there are tests asserting exactly that.
- **Invariants respected.** `InverseFoldingInput` and `ScoringInput` are untouched (diffs show only additions); `MPNNInput`/`LigandMPNNPackerInput` are scoped to their category modules; both flat `TOOL_REGISTRY` entries removed with tests asserting absence from `TOOL_REGISTRY` + presence in `CATALOG`/`TOOL_RUNNERS`; two-Tools-one-runner wiring verified (both map to `MPNNRunner`).
- **Empty-mutations change is safe.** `mutations: list[str]` is now required, so Pydantic enforces list-of-str at construction — the dropped `isinstance` check is genuinely redundant. Empty `[]` is still caught by `if not mutations`, and the message no longer misleadingly references the `extra` dict. Test updated to match the new message.
- Good breadth: `info` snapshots assert `x-autobio` hints (widget/tier), `output_schema` presence, notes, and specifically the ligandmpnn ligand note.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

1. **Dead constants — `src/autobio/tools/ligandmpnn_packer.py:54-60`.** `_DEFAULT_NUM_PACKS = 4`, `_DEFAULT_NUM_DENOISING_STEPS = 3`, `_DEFAULT_NUM_SAMPLES = 16` are now referenced nowhere (grep across `src/` and `tests/` returns only their definitions). Their values were promoted to `LigandMPNNPackerInput` field defaults, so these now silently duplicate the schema defaults — a drift hazard (update one, forget the other). `ruff` can't catch this (F841 only covers local assignments, not module-level constants), which is why it passed clean. **Root cause is a plan bug:** the Task 2 brief explicitly said "Keep ... `_DEFAULT_*`", but that instruction predated the field-default promotion and left them dead. Fix: delete lines 54-60 (the three constants + the "Default packing parameters" comment header).

2. **Shadow-rejection tests don't fully discriminate the two `_apply_extra` branches.** The shared error message always contains both `"collide with typed input fields"` and `"runner-derived config keys"` regardless of which condition fired, so matching on either substring can't prove the intended branch triggered. Net effect is small and asymmetric:
   - **mpnn is fine** — the `num_sequences` parametrization isolates the typed-field branch (it is *not* a config key, only `number_of_batches` is), so that path is genuinely exercised in isolation.
   - **packer can't isolate the typed-field branch** — every packer typed field (`mutations`/`num_packs`/…) is *also* a written config key, so both parametrized keys would still be caught by `key in config` even if the typed-field check were deleted. This is inherent to the packer schema (all typed fields map 1:1 to config keys), so the distinction is functionally moot there.
   
   Optional strengthening: add `match=extra_key` (assert the specific colliding key name, which the message does include) to make the assertion discriminate. Low value; safe to defer. This is pre-existing shared `base.py` behavior across all migrations, not introduced here.

### Recommendations

- Delete the three dead `_DEFAULT_*` constants (2-minute change) — it's the only thing standing between this branch and clean. If the team prefers, it fits naturally into the already-planned teardown pass, but shipping known dead code that shadows the real defaults is worth avoiding now.
- Consider correcting the "keep `_DEFAULT_*`" instruction in the plan template / any sibling plans so the same dead-code pattern isn't reproduced in later migrations that promote extra-dict defaults to typed fields.

### Assessment

**Ready to merge?** With fixes

**Reasoning:** The migration is correct, byte-compat is preserved and rigorously pinned by full-dict tests, `_apply_extra` is adopted correctly with production wiring confirmed, and all plan invariants hold — the only blocker is a trivial 3-line dead-constant deletion (a plan-instruction artifact); the test-discrimination point is a defer-able minor.</result>

# Code Review: Tools→Modes Plan 4f (RunMetadata.mode + rosetta migration)

### Strengths

- **Byte-compat testing is exemplary.** `TestRosettaByteCompatConfig` ships a full-dict equality test per mode PLUS an explicit `list(cfg.keys()) == list(expected.keys())` order assertion — for all four modes and both flexddg branches (`mutation_list` vs `resfile_path`). This is exactly the right way to lock a byte-compat invariant, and it directly encodes the per-mode config contract from the plan.
- **The config-build order is verifiably preserved.** New order (`binary, protocol, structure_path, database_path, score_function, out_dir, nstruct, [xml_path], [mutations, chains_to_move, mutation_list|resfile_path], …extra`) matches the pre-migration flat runner exactly. The one conditional in the old `_prepare_mutations` (`if chains: config["chains_to_move"]=…`) is now unconditional, but since validation has always required non-empty `chains_to_move` for flexddg, key order is identical for every valid input. `score_function`/`nstruct` were always-written in the old code, so promoting them to typed fields is byte-compat.
- **Multi-mode dispatch is clean and correct.** Mode-keyed `_MODE_CONFIG`, per-mode input subclasses (`RosettaBaseInput`/`RosettaRelaxInput`/`RosettaFlexDdgInput`), and dispatch via `self.current_mode.name`. `prepare_workspace` asserts `isinstance(input_data, RosettaBaseInput)` and narrows to `RosettaFlexDdgInput` inside the flexddg branch — mypy-sound and defensive. Validation runs before config assembly, so empty mutations/chains fail fast.
- **Per-mode image_tag + timeout resolve correctly** through the already-established `_image_tag()`/`_default_timeout()` (mode override → tool fallback). Tests pin both: 4 distinct image tags and the exact 600/3600/1800/14400 timeouts.
- **`RunMetadata.mode` is properly additive** (`str | None = None`, inserted after `tool_name`; Pydantic construction is kwarg-only so mid-struct insertion is safe) and correctly threaded via the `_build_metadata` ternary. Both branches of that ternary are directly tested (`test_build_metadata_mode_reflects_current_mode`), and the full `run()` lifecycle metadata is asserted per mode.
- **`_apply_extra` adoption is a strict improvement.** Power-user knobs (`backrub_trials`, `max_minimization_iter`, `ex1`/`ex2`) still merge; collisions with typed fields AND runner-derived config keys now fail fast — covered by both `test_extra_shadowing_typed_field_rejected` and `test_extra_shadowing_config_key_rejected`.
- **`ScoringInput` untouched; `TOOL_RUNNERS` collapse is complete.** Source tree grep confirms zero `rosetta_*` / `_VARIANT_CONFIG` / `_CONSUMED_EXTRA_KEYS` references in `rosetta.py` (the remaining hits are in the not-yet-migrated evoef2/complexa/openmm/antibody_lm — the next plans). Tests assert the 4 old keys are gone from both registries and `get_runner("rosetta_score")` raises.
- **Sound, reusable precedent for evoef2/complexa.** This is the first tool needing genuinely distinct per-mode images, and it does so purely through `Mode.image_tag` overrides with no infrastructure changes — the pattern (base input + subclasses, mode config table, `_apply_extra`, per-mode byte-compat tests) transfers directly to the next migrations.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None. No correctness defects found. The behavior changes below are intentional and plan-sanctioned; flagging only for release-notes visibility.

- **Interface breaks worth capturing in a migration note (not this PR's blocker).** Three caller-visible changes ship together: (a) the `chains` alias for `chains_to_move` is dropped; (b) `mode=` is now required to reach non-default modes (`get_runner("rosetta_relax")` is gone); (c) passing `score_function`/`nstruct`/other config keys via `extra` now *raises* instead of being silently consumed/merged. All are per the plan and strictly safer, but they belong in the README/migration-notes rewrite already tracked on the teardown backlog. Same deferral pattern as prior migrations — acceptable to defer, but don't lose it.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `tests/unit/test_rosetta.py:1246` — `_input_for_mode` lacks a return type annotation and leans on `# type: ignore[arg-type]` at three call sites. A `-&gt; RosettaBaseInput` return annotation (the common supertype) would document intent; the `type: ignore`s stem from `**kwargs: object`. Test-hygiene nit (already known).
- `src/autobio/tools/rosetta.py:45` — `_ROSETTA_BIN` is dead (defined, referenced nowhere). Pre-existing; safe to delete in the teardown pass.
- `src/autobio/tools/rosetta.py:52` / tests — `_MODE_CONFIG` keys and `ROSETTA_TOOL.modes` keys are coupled by convention but nothing cross-checks them; `_MODES` in the test is a hardcoded tuple rather than derived from the Tool. A one-line `set(_MODE_CONFIG) == set(get_tool("rosetta").modes)` guard would catch a future mode added to only one place. Future-proofing only — current parametrized run tests would still surface a KeyError.
- `tests/unit/test_rosetta.py:1924-1934` — the info-snapshot test indexes `parsed["modes"][0]`/`[3]`; slightly brittle, though it also asserts the full ordered name list, so an ordering regression would still be caught.

### Recommendations
- Merge as-is. When the tracked README/teardown item is done, fold in the rosetta migration note (mode-based invocation, dropped `chains` alias, extra-shadowing now rejected) and delete `_ROSETTA_BIN`.
- For the evoef2/complexa (Plan 4g) migrations, reuse this PR's byte-compat test shape verbatim (full-dict + explicit key-order assertion per mode) — it's the strongest guard in this change.

### Assessment
**Ready to merge?** Yes

**Reasoning:** The migration faithfully implements every plan invariant — per-mode byte-compat config (keys and order, both flexddg branches), per-mode image/timeout, additive+threaded `RunMetadata.mode`, `ScoringInput` untouched, `_apply_extra` collision rejection, and a complete `TOOL_RUNNERS` collapse — with meaningful, high-coverage tests and no correctness defects; the only open items are the already-triaged minors and a doc/migration-note deferral.</result>

## Code Review: Plan 4g (evoef2 + complexa → catalog Tools)

### Strengths

- **Byte-compat is airtight for both tools.** I independently traced every per-mode config-build path against the pre-migration code:
  - **evoef2**: dict order `command → structure_path → evoef2_bin → out_dir`, then the binding branch (`repair` always, `split_chains` only if truthy) or the build_mutant branch (`mutations`, then `mutant_file` after `_write_mutation_file`), then `_apply_extra`. Identical order to the old `_VARIANT_CONFIG` flow. The `repair` default (True) and the `if input_data.split_chains:` truthiness gate both match the old `extra.get("repair", True)` / `if split_chains:` semantics.
  - **complexa**: `variant → pipeline_config → ckpt_name → ae_ckpt_name → weights_dir → design_specs → n_batches → out_dir`, then extra. `config["variant"] = mode` reproduces the old `_VARIANT_CONFIG[...]["variant"]` values exactly (protein_binder/ligand_binder/ame), and the per-mode ckpt/pipeline strings are copied verbatim.
- **The byte-compat tests are genuinely strong, not decorative.** Full-dict equality PLUS an explicit `list(cfg.keys()) == list(expected.keys())` order assertion per mode, including the two binding variants (split_chains present vs. absent) and build_mutant's `mutant_file`. This is exactly the rosetta test shape and it would catch key-order or key-set drift.
- **Sound, well-differentiated input-modeling choices.** evoef2's per-mode subclasses (`EvoEF2Base/Repair/Binding/BuildMutant`) correctly capture genuinely disjoint fields with different requiredness (`mutations` required only for build_mutant) — freesasa pattern. complexa's single shared `ComplexaInput` with the `design_specs` escape-hatch is the right call since the per-mode differences live entirely inside the free-form spec dict — rfd3 pattern. Both match their cited exemplars.
- **`_apply_extra` adoption is correct and a real hardening.** Both runners drop their manual consumed-keys loops. For normal per-mode invocation, config output is unchanged; the new behavior additionally *fails fast* when `extra` shadows a typed field or a runner-derived key (previously silent overwrite for complexa, whose consumed set was empty). Tests cover both rejection (`n_batches`/`repair`) and pass-through (`custom_flag`, and complexa's container-level `mode`).
- **Registry/runner collapse is complete and correct.** `TOOL_RUNNERS` drops the 5 retired flat keys and keeps/adds the two canonical names; the diff and the `get_tool`/`TOOL_REGISTRY`/`TOOL_RUNNERS` assertions confirm all 6 flat names are gone and only `evoef2`/`complexa` remain. `mutations` container-contract key preserved. `ScoringInput`/`StructureDesignInput` bodies untouched (schema files only *add* classes).
- **Info-snapshot tests verify the notes rewording requirement**: `not any("extra[" in note ...)` per mode plus `x-autobio` widget hints — directly proves the `extra['...']`→typed-field reword landed and `output_schema` renders per mode.
- **Clean teardown setup.** With complexa migrated, `StructureDesignInput` is confirmed to have zero production consumers, and the naming-collision comment (catalog `Mode` vs Complexa's `config["mode"]`) is documented in the module docstring — good breadcrumb for the next plan.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None. Byte-compat holds, dispatch is on `self.current_mode.name`, the collapse is complete, and the `_apply_extra` behavior changes are plan-sanctioned hardening that only affect abnormal (shadowing) inputs.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- **`tests/unit/test_evoef2_e2e.py` — `_run_e2e` lost its `input_data` type annotation** (the param is now bare `input_data,`). Since all three mode inputs inherit from `EvoEF2BaseInput`, annotate it `input_data: EvoEF2BaseInput` to restore type coverage. mypy passes only because missing annotations aren't flagged in non-strict mode. Trivial fix.
- **Stale "variant" wording in integration comments/docstrings** (e.g. `tests/integration/test_complexa_integration.py` section divider `# Ligand binder variant — generate mode`). Cosmetic; fold into the README/teardown wording sweep.
- **`# noqa: TC001 - needed at runtime for isinstance` on the `BaseInput` import** (both `evoef2.py:26` and `complexa.py:41`) is technically inaccurate — the runtime `isinstance` checks use the concrete subclasses (`EvoEF2BaseInput`/`ComplexaInput`), not `BaseInput`, which appears only in the (string, un-evaluated under `from __future__ import annotations`) `prepare_workspace` param annotation. This line is pre-existing and unchanged by this diff, and it's a codebase-wide convention, so defer — but the comment reason is worth correcting during teardown.
- **Behavior nuance worth noting (not a bug):** for a mode that doesn't own a formerly-global-consumed key, that key now passes through instead of being silently dropped — e.g. passing `repair` via `extra` in *repair* mode now writes `config["repair"]` (old `_CONSUMED_EXTRA_KEYS` dropped it globally). This is nonsensical usage, is plan-sanctioned (delete consumed-keys sets, adopt `_apply_extra`), harmless container-side, and arguably more correct. No action needed.

### Recommendations
- Apply the one-line `_run_e2e` annotation fix now (zero risk); batch the comment-wording cleanups (variant→mode, TC001 reason) into the already-planned teardown/README rewrite alongside the cross-doc flat-name references.
- No new tests required; coverage is thorough. If you want belt-and-suspenders, one test asserting evoef2 `Mode.supports_batch is False` would lock in the default I verified, but it's optional.

### Assessment
**Ready to merge?** Yes

**Reasoning:** Byte-compat config.json (keys and order) is preserved per mode for both tools with strong equality+order tests; dispatch, the `_apply_extra` hardening, the `TOOL_RUNNERS` collapse, the untouched `ScoringInput`/`StructureDesignInput`, and the `mutations` contract key are all correct, and the migration cleanly frees `StructureDesignInput` for teardown. The only findings are cosmetic/deferred minors (a dropped test annotation, stale "variant" comments, an inaccurate but pre-existing noqa reason) — none block merge.</result>

Summary:
- **Different categories per mode**: design=`INVERSE_FOLDING` (Tool primary), score=`SCORING` — requires a `Mode.category` override on the score mode; genuinely cross-category, not just cosmetic.
- **Same image_tag** for both modes (`esm-if1:1.0.0`) — no per-mode image override needed, unlike rosetta.

- **`extra` handling**: both classes currently do unfiltered `config.update(input_data.extra)` — must switch to `self._apply_extra(config, input_data)` per spec §3.4, which also requires adding a collision-rejection unit test per mode (none exists today).
- **Key consolidation risk**: output-schema/parse_output divergence is total (different result-JSON top-level keys, different item models, different wrapper types) — the merged runner should dispatch with a simple `if/else` on `self.current_mode.name` (à la FreeSASA's two-branch shape) rather than forcing a Rosetta-style shared config table; also both classes currently have zero host-side validation (no file-existence/PDB-suffix/sequences-required checks), which is a gap worth optionally closing to match sibling tools (rosetta/freesasa) during the same change. Container side (`validate_config.sh`/`run_esm_if1.py`) already keys on `mode ∈ {design, score}` — no key-name translation needed, and `containers/` should not be touched.</result>

Key findings:

1. **`TOOL_REGISTRY`/`ToolEntry`**: `ToolCategory` is fully independent and must stay; `ToolEntry`/`TOOL_REGISTRY`/`get_tool`/`list_tools` in `registry.py` can be deleted together. ## Task Quality Review: Task 3 — Runner refactors (esm_if1/antifold staging, dead `_ROSETTA_BIN`, rfd3/complexa dedup)

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ **3a (esm_if1/antifold staging hoist + explicit guards).** Diffed both files against `881a862` line-by-line: the 3-line `shutil.copy2` block moved verbatim above the `if mode == "design":` branch with zero changes to its contents; the two per-branch copies were deleted, nothing else. `else: # "score"` → `else:` + `assert self.current_mode.name == "score", self.current_mode.name` is the *only* added line in each score branch, in both `prepare_workspace` and `parse_output`, in both files. - ✅ **Antifold's `_validate_chain_ids(input_data)`** call is untouched, still unconditional, still runs before the (now-hoisted) copy and before the mode branch.
- ✅ **3b (`_ROSETTA_BIN` deletion).** Only the one line (`rosetta.py:44` pre-branch) removed. `_ROSETTA_DB` still present at line 44 and still used at line 109 (`"database_path": _ROSETTA_DB`). No other rosetta.py change (confirmed via `diff` against `881a862`).
- ✅ **3c (rfd3/complexa dedup).** `_check_input_references` is a `@staticmethod` per-file (not shared across files), called from both `prepare_workspace`'s rewrite loop and `_validate_inputs` in each of `rfd3.py` and `complexa.py`. Error message string is byte-identical to the pre-branch `_validate_inputs` wording in both files.
- ✅ **Scope.** `git diff --stat 881a862 e41507a` shows exactly the 5 listed files (antifold.py, complexa.py, esm_if1.py, rfd3.py, rosetta.py), matching the review package's diffstat exactly. No tests, containers/, docs, or schemas touched — confirmed no test files differ between the two commits.

### Risk-by-risk verification
1. **Byte-compat config.** Confirmed by direct diff against `git show 881a862:...` — only line *movement* (copy hoisted, `else:`/`assert` added), no change to dict contents or key order. No config-affecting behavior change.
2. **Explicit asserts.** Present exactly as specified in both files, both methods; nothing else touched in the score branches or `parse_output` bodies.
3. **`_ROSETTA_BIN`/`_ROSETTA_DB`.** Confirmed — dead constant removed, live constant and its one use-site untouched.
4. **rfd3/complexa message consolidation — the flagged nuance.** Verified independently, not just taken on the implementer's word: in *both* pre-branch files, `prepare_workspace`'s very first statement is `self._validate_inputs(input_data)`, called before the copy/rewrite loop that contained the (now-deleted) second, unsorted-list-formatted check. Since `_validate_inputs` always runs first and raises before the rewrite loop can be reached, the `prepare_workspace`-local check was provably unreachable in every call path (`run()` calls `prepare_workspace` directly; there's no separate path that skips `_validate_inputs`). The consolidated helper's message is verbatim the `_validate_inputs` wording. `test_rfd3.py::test_unreferenced_spec_input_raises` and `test_complexa.py::test_unreferenced_spec_input_raises` both call `prepare_workspace` directly and assert only `match="no matching file"` (not the exact "Provided files" formatting), so they were not sensitive to the sorted/unsorted distinction either before or after — they pass unchanged. ## Issues

**Critical — TOOL_SPEC.md:589 (`input_format`), pre-existing but carried forward through this rewrite**
The doc states: *"`input_format`, where used, is a tuple of strings documenting the tool's native input format (on `Mode`, alongside `notes`)... surfaced by `autobio info &lt;tool&gt;` as a separate 'Input Format' section (table mode) or `"input_format"` key (JSON mode) per mode."* This field does **not exist**:
- `Mode` in `src/autobio/core/catalog.py:20-37` has no `input_format` field (only `name, display_name, description, input_schema, output_schema, default_timeout, supports_batch, image_tag, category, notes`).
- `format_tool_info_catalog()` in `src/autobio/cli/formatters.py:85-152` never emits an "Input Format" section/key.
- It's not used by any real `Tool`/`Mode` registration in the codebase (confirmed by grepping `input_format` across `src/`).
This field was a leftover from the pre-refactor `ToolEntry` (confirmed in `docs/REFACTOR.md:48` and the design spec, both of which list `input_format` as a *legacy* `ToolEntry` attribute that the new `Mode` dataclass explicitly dropped). The task's diff (`git diff 38c6aa4..81bf888` line ~985/590) only reworded this paragraph to say "on `Mode`" instead of removing it or flagging it as unimplemented — so it now asserts, more strongly than before, that this is current `Mode` API. A developer following this instruction would write `Mode(..., input_format=(...))` and get `TypeError: Mode.__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'input_format'`. This falls squarely inside Task 5's mandate to make TOOL_SPEC.md API-accurate against the real catalog.

**Minor — TOOL_SPEC.md:439 (dispatch-style naming)**
"*Dict-driven dispatch (`rosetta.py`, `evoef2.py`, `complexa.py`, `openmm.py`) ... looked up once via `_MODE_CONFIG[self.current_mode.name]`*" is only literally true for `rosetta.py`/`complexa.py`. `openmm.py` uses `_VARIANT_CONFIG` (confirmed in its own module docstring and code), and `evoef2.py` uses a small `_MODE_COMMAND` dict for the command string plus separate `if mode == "binding"` / `if mode == "build_mutant"` blocks for the rest of the config (a hybrid, not "looked up once"). A developer grepping `openmm.py`/`evoef2.py` for `_MODE_CONFIG` per this doc's guidance won't find it. Low impact (informational aside, not the core walkthrough), but still an inaccurate code-identifier claim.

**Minor — CLAUDE.md drift (out of Task 5's scope, noted for follow-up)**
The root `CLAUDE.md` "Adding a New Tool" summary (lines 70, 86) still says *"Register a `ToolEntry` in `TOOL_REGISTRY` at module level"* — stale relative to the now-correct TOOL_SPEC.md. `CLAUDE.md` isn't in Task 5's file list and wasn't touched (correctly, per boundary rules), but the brief's instruction to "align with the current CLAUDE.md" summary is now backwards — TOOL_SPEC.md is accurate and CLAUDE.md is stale. Worth a follow-up task to update CLAUDE.md's summary to match.

## Summary

I traced the boltz (working), openfold3, and chai template/MSA plumbing end-to-end, including pulling actual vendored source (`openfold-3`, `chai_lab==0.6.1`) out of the already-built local Docker images (`autobio-openfold3:1.0.0`, `ghcr.io/briney/autobio-chai:1.0.0`) to determine ground truth about what the underlying ML tools actually support — not just what autobio's wrapper code does.

Fact sheet written to: `/home/briney/git/autobio/.superpowers/sdd/recon/templates_msa.md` (git-ignored per `.superpowers/sdd/.gitignore`).

Key findings, most important first:

- **boltz (reference)**: `_build_boltz_yaml` in `src/autobio/tools/boltz.py` writes templates as a top-level YAML `templates: [{cif: ...}]` list and MSAs as a per-chain `msa:` key inside the native Boltz YAML — the upstream `boltz` CLI (`containers/boltz/run.sh`) reads that YAML natively. No synthesis needed; autobio just copies files and rewrites paths.

- **openfold3 `msa_paths` — feasible fix.** The real `InferenceQuerySet.Chain` model (found in the container image) has `main_msa_file_paths`/`paired_msa_file_paths` fields that genuinely accept user `.a3m`/`.npz`/`.sto` files per chain. `src/autobio/tools/openfold3.py`'s `_build_query_json` (lines ~161-227) never reads `input_data.msa_paths` at all — this is a straightforward wiring gap, fixable the same way boltz does it (chain-ID-prefixed filenames → per-chain field).

- **openfold3 `templates` and chai `templates` — NOT simply wireable.** Both underlying tools only accept a template-search *hits/alignment file* (`.a3m`/`.sto`/`.m8`, containing real sequence-alignment data against PDB-ID-identified structures), not a raw user-supplied structure file. This is confirmed directly from vendored source: OpenFold3's `TemplatePreprocessorSettings` + `Chain.template_alignment_file_path`/`template_entry_chain_ids`, and Chai-1's `run_inference(template_hits_path=...)` → `parse_m8_to_template_hits(..., template_cif_folder=...)`. Producing this file requires actually running a homology/alignment pipeline — real new engineering, not a config-key fix. `containers/chai/run_chai.py` also never reads a template config key at all, despite `chai_lab` exposing `template_hits_path`.

- Both `OpenFold3Input.templates`/`.msa_paths` and `Chai1Input.templates` are copied into the workspace and validated (file-exists checks) but never referenced by `_build_query_json`/config — confirmed by `tests/unit/test_openfold3.py::test_templates_copied` and `tests/unit/test_chai.py::test_templates_copied`, which explicitly assert `"templates" not in cfg`, i.e. the bug is currently encoded as expected test behavior.

- Byte-compat risk: `test_config_full_dict_equality_minimal`/`_with_overrides` in both `test_openfold3.py` (lines 411-457) and `test_chai.py` (lines 327-380) do exact `assert cfg == {...}` — any fix must be audited against these and likely needs new cases added for the `msa_paths`-provided scenario.

- No integration test in the repo (`test_openfold3_integration.py`, `test_chai_integration.py`, `test_boltz_integration.py`) touches templates or MSA paths at all — a pre-existing coverage gap worth flagging for the fix's test plan.

## Final Whole-Branch Review: openfold3/chai templates + MSA bug fix (cf5d72c..108fd41)

### Strengths

- **Wiring exactly matches the ground-truth API.** `_build_query_json` sets `chain["main_msa_file_paths"] = ["/workspace/inputs/&lt;name&gt;"]` keyed by filename-stem→chain-id, which recon §2 confirms is the genuine `Chain.main_msa_file_paths` field on OpenFold3's query-JSON model. The implementation is byte-for-byte the same pattern as the boltz reference (`src/autobio/tools/boltz.py:213-218` — `msa_path.stem` key, `/workspace/inputs/{msa_path.name}` value), so it's consistent with the established working convention.
- **MSA is correctly scoped to polymer chains only.** The wiring lives inside the `etype in ("protein","dna","rna")` branch; ligand chains never receive `main_msa_file_paths`. Verified end-to-end: a protein+ligand input produced the MSA key on chain A and nothing on ligand L.
- **Conditional wiring preserves byte-compat.** With no `msa_paths`, `main_msa_file_paths` is absent from the chain dict (verified directly: keys = `['molecule_type','chain_ids','sequence']`). Existing equality tests (`test_config_full_dict_equality_*`, `test_generated_query_json_equality_protein_ligand_noncanonical`) pass unchanged.
- **Mutual-exclusion is correctly ordered.** In `_validate_inputs` (`openfold3.py:238-248`) the `use_msa_server=True` check fires *before* the file-existence loop, so a bad combination is rejected regardless of whether files exist. The adapted `test_missing_msa_file_raises` correctly sets `use_msa_server=False` so it still exercises the existence path (test not weakened).
- **Documentation is now honest.** `grep "use the 'templates' field" src/` → zero. `_OPENFOLD3_NOTES` now states "User-supplied template structure files are not supported" and accurately describes server-side `use_templates`. The `msa_paths` field description documents the stem convention + `use_msa_server=False` requirement.
- **Removal is complete and correctly bounded.** `OpenFold3Input.templates` and `Chai1Input.templates` gone, along with both host copy blocks and both existence-validation blocks. `ESMFoldInput.templates` (line 95), `BoltzInput.templates` (line 120), `OpenFold3Input.use_templates` (line 300), `Chai1Input.msa_directory`, and the notes-only `use_templates_server` passthrough are all intact. No dangling references.
- **Coherent boundary.** Exactly 6 files changed (plan doc + `structure_prediction.py` + `openfold3.py` + `chai.py` + 2 test files); `git diff --name-only cf5d72c..108fd41 -- containers/` is empty. The two tasks compose cleanly: msa_paths works, templates fields honestly removed, docs accurate.

### Verification results
- `python -m pytest tests/unit/test_openfold3.py tests/unit/test_chai.py tests/unit/test_boltz.py tests/unit/test_esmfold.py -q` → exit 0
- Full `python -m pytest -m "not docker and not gpu" -q` → exit 0
- `ruff check src/ tests/` → All checks passed; `ruff format --check` → already formatted
- `mypy src/` → Success, no issues (57 files)
- End-to-end: constructed `OpenFold3Input(sequences={A,L}, msa_paths=[A.a3m], use_msa_server=False)` → query.json wires `main_msa_file_paths` onto A only; mutex raises the exact message; no-msa input omits the key.
- No `templates=[...]` list usage remains in tests (only the valid `use_templates=False` toggle); all `*_via_extra_rejected` guard tests retained; new substantive tests present (`test_msa_files_not_wired_when_no_matching_chain`, `test_generated_query_json_equality_with_msa_paths`, `test_msa_paths_with_use_msa_server_raises`).

### Issues

**Critical:** None.

**Important:** None.

**Minor (informational, non-blocking):**
- `src/autobio/schemas/base.py:28` — `BaseInput` uses Pydantic's default `extra="ignore"`, so a caller still passing the now-removed `templates=[...]` kwarg has it silently dropped rather than erroring. Separately, `extra={"templates": ...}` would now pass `_apply_extra` (no longer a typed-field collision) and be forwarded verbatim into `config.json`, where the container ignores it. Both are benign: the field never functioned (previously copied-but-ignored → template-free prediction; now dropped → identical template-free prediction), and forwarding unrecognized `extra` keys is the documented contract. Worth awareness only; no fix needed for this branch.
- `openfold3.py:303` note says MSA "filenames should start with the chain ID" while the actual logic (and the more precise field description at `structure_prediction.py:281`) is stem-*equality*. This imprecision is copied verbatim from the boltz note (`boltz.py:277`), so it's intentionally consistent with the reference rather than a new defect.

### Assessment

Both tasks were executed exactly to plan and to the recon's feasibility verdict. The one genuinely wireable field (`openfold3.msa_paths`) is wired correctly against the real container API with sound mutual-exclusion and preserved byte-compat; the two inert `templates` fields are removed cleanly with the false documentation corrected; no `containers/` changes. All required verifications pass.

**Ready to merge?** Yes

**Reasoning:** The fix is correct, complete, and honest — msa_paths now genuinely reaches the container via `main_msa_file_paths`, the never-functional templates fields are removed with accurate docs, and the full suite/ruff/mypy are green with no regressions. The only notes are benign informational observations about pass-through semantics.</result>
